‘I want to…’ – Virgil van Dijk issues Liverpool contract hint as mega decision awaits

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Virgil van Dijk has spoken about his long-term future at Liverpool, where he now has less than eight months remaining on his current contract.

Having penned a two-year extension in April 2025, the club captain is once again approaching a major crossroads in his illustrious career as the clock ticks towards the juncture at which a decision will have to be made.

Fellow stalwart Alisson Becker has voiced his desire to remain with the Reds beyond next summer, with his agent in ‘preliminary talks‘ over a potential new deal for the goalkeeper, although the Dutch defender has been rather more coy about his own situation.

Van Dijk addressed his long-term future when speaking to reporters after Liverpool’s 3-1 victory at Crystal Palace.

In quotes published by the Liverpool Echo on Tuesday, the 35-year-old said: “I feel good but it’s not about me at the moment. Let’s see. I’m not in any panic whatsoever. I want to keep playing and continue what I’m doing.

“Let’s see what the season brings. I’ve had an amazing time already so far. If that will be extended, that’s the case. If not, then I will go somewhere else. There’s nothing to report at the moment but that doesn’t mean that there’s any stress or anything.”

(Photo by Justin Setterfield/Getty Images)

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

Publicly at least, Van Dijk is giving nothing away about his future, with the Liverpool captain seemingly happy to stay on but also keeping his options open in case a new deal with the Reds can’t be agreed upon.

It’d certainly represent a break from the norm from Anfield chiefs to offer a lucrative contract extension to a player who’s already on £350,000 per week (Capology) and who’ll turn 36 next summer, but we’re not talking about any ordinary footballer here.

The captain is one of the greatest to ever pull on the iconic red shirt, and aside from an uncharacteristic error against Villarreal last month, he’s been imperious alongside Jeremy Jacquet at the heart of our defence this season.

The Frenchman is among several young centre-backs in line to ultimately succeed Van Dijk as our defensive general in the years to come (along with Giovanni Leoni and Ifeanyi Ndukwe), but the influence of the experienced Dutchman is still clear.

If the skipper’s preference would be to continue with Liverpool into next season, then hopefully he and the club can reach an agreement which’d keep him at Anfield beyond the end of this campaign.

Smart meters to cost the UK billions: 2G switch-off means replacing 12-13 million

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Sunset of legacy cellphone tech means forced upgrade for many millions of smart energy meter users

Between 12 and 13 million UK smart meters could stop working properly when  2G and 3G networks are switched off by 2033, and replacing them all is likely to cost around £4 billion ($5.3 billion).

Smart meters have long been a controversial topic thanks to the British government's botched rollout of the technology; they were supposed to be fitted into 80 percent of households by 2020.

But numerous delays and technical hitches, such as consumers finding their meter stopped being smart if they switched energy supplier, hindered progress.

Now, a report from connectivity biz Wirepas, "The European Connectivity Trilemma," shows that it isn't just Brits who have problems, but that other European countries also have their share of smart meter issues and that no two countries have adopted the same approach.

The UK's issues center on the fact that early meters used 2G or 3G cellular connections to transmit energy usage back to the supplier. These continued to be fitted until 4G versions began arriving at the end of 2024, the report says.

This issue was covered by The Register back in 2023, and again in 2024.

However, the government had already announced measures to phase out 2G and 3G networks by 2033 several years earlier, back in 2021, so it was known that any meters being fitted would have a limited lifespan.

In fact, 3G services have now already been withdrawn by the networks, and they all plan to phase out 2G ahead of the 2033 deadline. BT/EE will begin closing its network from May 2029, VMO2 will start the same summer, and Vodafone aims to switch off in spring 2030.

The report claims the UK now faces two substantial areas of expenditure as a result. The first covers newer, second generation (SMETS2) meters, where only the communications module needs swapping out, but even this is likely to cost £1.3 to £1.4 billion (c $1.7 to $1.9 billion) before the hardware cost of each new hub is even factored in.

The other is for an estimated 7 million older, first-generation (SMETS1) meter installations, which have the cellular comms hard-wired in, so the whole meter typically needs replacing. The report authors estimate this will cost another £2.1 billion to £2.45 billion ($2.8 billion to $3.3 billion), at an indicative replacement cost of £300 to £350 per installation.

This results in the estimated £4 billion in gross replacement costs, which will ultimately feed into household energy bills, adding to the very costs the government is currently trying to reduce.

We asked the Department for Energy Security and Net Zero (DESNZ), which oversees smart meters, for comment.

While some of these older meters would naturally have been due for replacement as part of normal asset renewal cycles, the cell network shutdown makes the replacement program unavoidable and dictates when much of the expenditure must occur.

According to the report, the SMETS2 replacement largely applies to central and southern Britain, as second-gen meters in the north use a long-range radio network and are not subject to the 2G/3G cut-off. However, the issue of older SMETS1 meters applies nationwide.

Wirepas' interest in all of this is that it specializes in IoT connectivity and is pushing NR+ as a solution to the issue. Based on the DECT-2020 New Radio standard developed by the European Telecommunication Standards Institute (ETSI), this is designed to be scalable and operates in the license-exempt and globally available 1.9GHz part of the spectrum.

When 4G technology reaches the end of its life, (which isn't likely to happen until the 2040s, and there is no planned date) the UK's smart meters will need to be replaced once again, the report asserts. However, this is sufficiently far in the future that it is unlikely to trouble policymakers anytime soon. ®

IAN HERBERT: Classless Chuckle Brothers Jason Wilcox and Omar Berrada are not fit to lace Michael Carrick's boots - they have treated a dignified Man United legend shamefully with this painful charade

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Published: 07:34 EST, 10 November 2026 | Updated: 07:49 EST, 10 November 2026

Both Michael Carrick and the man who has decided to sack him were present on Saturday night at a 40th anniversary dinner for Sir Alex Ferguson, staged in the dining suite where the Old Trafford dressing rooms used to be. It was lost on no one that Carrick, oozing class and humour that evening, spoke far better than Omar Berrada, Manchester United’s chief executive.

There were 100 or so present and Carrick had just been through the wringer of the 3-2 win over Roma but he did not let his predicament obscure the bigger picture. Carrick ‘spoke brilliantly’, actor James Nesbitt, who was also present, related. ‘The Roma result had just happened, but he showed a real gratitude to Sir Alex. He spoke with confidence and humour, but also with a real understanding of the enormity of what he achieved.’

Those who really understand United would not have been surprised. In the days when it was a classy, brilliantly run club, Carrick was the one who, perhaps more than any other senior player, would always look out for the rank-and-file staff – ensuring that the backroom teams were taken out for a drink at the end of the season, and that taxis home were laid on.

It was he who latched on to what was good about the Manchester United ‘family,’ ensuring that everyone from the reception staff to the laundry team were made to feel a part of it. Sir Jim Ratcliffe’s Ineos have clinically dismantled the old sense of a United ‘community' but Carrick’s return created a gratifying sense around Old Trafford, back in January, that it was perhaps not entirely dead.

All of which contributes to the unalloyed rage that many who love the club will be feeling today about the shameful and classless treatment of Carrick. A man forced through the ritual of driving into the training ground at 7.15am this morning by Berrada and United director of football Jason Wilcox – men not fit to lace his boots – when the whole world knows he is toast.

As Carrick, a professional to his core, went through this painful charade, Berrada and Wilcox – the Statler and Waldorf of the modern Manchester United – had had their secretaries arrange air travel to Monaco today for a meeting with Ratcliffe, which could not be brought forward.

Michael Carrick oozes class and humility but has been dragged through a painful charade by his superiors

Omar Berrada (left) and Jason Wilcox (right) are the the Statler and Waldorf of the modern Manchester United

There have been moments amid the recent tempest which point to United requiring a more commanding manager for the depths that Ratcliffe and his dismal men have reduced the club to. I could see from my vantage point close to the United dugout at their trip to Chelsea, two weeks back, that Carrick simply didn’t have the words to steer the ship away from that afternoon’s disaster.

But in the faces of men like Kobbie Mainoo and, before injury, Marcus Rashford, who have been restored to life and self-belief as United players, we have also witnessed Carrick’s faculty to see and know quality in players and give it full force.

A year ago, we were witnessing Ruben Amorim, the manager hired at vast expense by Ratcliffe’s football geniuses, run down Mainoo’s United career. In the insane mayhem of Sunday afternoon's 4-4 draw against Aston Villa, he was the team’s creative anchor.

The people who have actually mattered at United – the fanbase – appreciate the respect Carrick warrants. It’s why the anthem carrying his name was still coming down from the Stretford End and the old North Stand on Sunday.

And so, of course, does Sir Alex, even though you half suspect he wondered all along if Carrick had quite the bite for the toughest job in world football, while hoping against hope he’d thrive.

‘There is a casualness about him that causes people to misunderstand his value and his constitution,’ Ferguson wrote of Carrick in his autobiography. ‘His handicap was, I feel, that he lacked the bravado of Frank Lampard and Steven Gerrard. But his character has always been outstanding. He's a football man.’ Sir Alex considered Carrick a better player than both those two.

And now what you suspect will be Carrick’s valedictory days at the club he has served so well are presided over by executives whose own fitness for purpose at Old Trafford was an open question from the start.

Very few people around Manchester City viewed the loss of Berrada, their ‘chief football officer’, as something to be fearful about, when Ratcliffe decided to make him United’s chief executive in 2024. Some former City insiders privately wondered how on earth he’d landed a job of that kind.

‘There is a casualness about him that causes people to misunderstand his value and his constitution,’ Sir Alex Ferguson wrote of Carrick in his autobiography

Sir Alex considered Carrick a better player than both Frank Lampard and Steven Gerrard

United want to tell Carrick ‘face to face’ what the whole world knows. These people won’t even discern what gross disrespect they are affording a man who deserves far better

And if Ratcliffe thought he was accessing City’s intellectual property when recruiting Jason Wilcox as technical director, then that was dodgy logic, too. Wilcox had only run City’s academy – a grossly incomparable challenge. His comparable experience to the United role was a mere 15 months as Southampton’s director of football. They were in the Championship for some of that time.

These were the men jetting out to Ratcliffe’s tax haven to tell him who should and should not manage United. Thus subjecting Carrick to another phantom training session at the Carrington – two weeks after watching the Under 21s play there while those Chuckle Brothers put their heads together about sacking him, across the complex.

United are putting it around, would you believe, that they want to be seen to be ‘doing the right thing’ and ‘observing the right process’. Well frankly, many inside football view this leadership group’s decision making as an embarrassment.

Nesbitt expressed sympathy for Carrick. ‘I feel for him,’ he said. ‘I'm no pundit but I am a fan. Clearly there's dissatisfaction with the fans about what we perceive is going on, but I think he's trying his best.’

United want to tell Carrick ‘face to face’ what the whole world knows. These people won’t even discern what gross disrespect they are affording a man who deserves far better.

President Trump says the FBI is investigating Jensen Huang’s affair

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

This raises questions about the company’s influence on the U.S. government.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

As more information about the ongoing situation at Nvidia unravels, U.S. President Donald Trump has confirmed that the FBI is investigating CEO Jensen Huang’s affair with a suspected Chinese spy days after he admitted to the indiscretion. According to the Associated Press, the president said this during a briefing at the White House, with one reporter asking him about his knowledge of Huang’s issue before it became publicly known. The news comes amidst a separate bombshell report from Bloomberg, which claims that Nvidia intervened in a US government probe of suspected chip smuggling by Megaspeed, where Alice Huang was an executive.

“Well, I’ve been hearing about it, and I’ve been reading about it over a period of time, but, uh, not too long ago,” Trump said. “We’ll look into, I know the FBI is looking into it in terms of something, and I think he’ll be in good shape.” He also said, “I did speak to him today just to see if he’s feeling alright, how you’re doing. He said he had absolutely no idea that the person in question was a spy.”

It has been previously reported that U.S. intelligence services suspected that Alice Huang, who previously held an executive position at Megaspeed, had ties to the Chinese Ministry of State Security (MSS), which is one of the country’s intelligence services, and that they told the President about it over a year ago. However, it was reported he did not act on the information, as he reportedly did not want to jeopardize Nvidia sales or antagonize Beijing, especially after the White House approved the export of H200 chips to China with an attached 25% fee.

Congress has called Huang once to confront him about these rumors, with the CEO confirming his affair with Alice Huang shortly after. The committee said it has "concerns over Nvidia’s security protocols against foreign adversary espionage and Mr. Huang’s reported ties to a Chinese intelligence-linked executive of a Singapore-based data center company.” Now, it seems that the FBI, which is the U.S.’s primary domestic counterintelligence agency, is looking into the situation, too.

While the infidelity of tech executives is usually outside the purview of politicians and law enforcement, Jensen Huang’s sensitive position as the head of the leading AI chip manufacturer that arguably single-handedly drove the global AI race has made him a prominent target. China has denied the accusation that Alice Huang is its agent, but a think tank suggests that “China is employing ‘attractive women’ posing as investors, business professionals, academics, or romantic partners to gain access to U.S. tech talent, networks, and research infrastructure.”

This is why Nvidia’s supposed intervention in the U.S. Bureau of Industry and Security’s (BIS) investigation into Megaspeed is becoming a concern. Megaspeed has been investigated for allegedly smuggling $2 billion worth of Nvidia AI processors into China, although Nvidia has claimed that it has done its due diligence and did not find any signs of diversions by the company. Despite that, Bloomberg reports that Nvidia executive vice president and general counsel Tim Teter reached out to U.S. Under Secretary of Commerce for Industry and Security and BIS head Jeffrey Kessler to ask about the administration’s investigation into the company and whether the BIS was involved in Singapore’s detention of Alice Huang at that time. Sources claimed that the U.S. agency was not involved in Ms. Huang’s apprehension by Singaporean authorities.

The publication reported BIS staff were told to route all Nvidia requests through Kessler or to seek approval from him for any outreach involving the company, which people say was highly unusual, and that Teter even supposedly reached out directly to BIS employees. When the affected employees forwarded the emails to Kessler and raised their concerns, the BIS chief instructed them to handle the case by the book. But in late 2025, the Justice Department, which was working alongside the BIS, asked the bureau to pause its efforts on the Megaspeed investigation.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

Even though Jensen Huang admitted to having an affair with Alice Huang, who was linked to Megaspeed and is suspected to be working at the behest of Beijing, there is still no clear proof that Ms. Huang had a direct role in influencing the Nvidia chief to push for the resumption of Nvidia AI chip sales to China. Still, the unraveling of this supposed spy plot has caught the attention of the American leadership, and they’re now asking questions about Nvidia’s security protocols and the company’s sway on Washington.

Mourinho hopes Endrick gets Brazil minutes as Real Madrid youngster weighs up future

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Jose Mourinho would be grateful to Carlo Ancelotti if the Brazil boss hands Endrick minutes over the international window.

Endrick enjoyed a fruitful loan spell at Lyon in the second half of last season, registering 16 goal involvements in 21 appearances (eight goals, eight assists).

However, the youngster has been on the fringes of Mourinho's Real Madrid squad this season, clocking up only 140 minutes across seven appearances.

Endrick has started just once for Los Blancos this term, but he has been called up by Ancelotti for Brazil's clashes with Japan and Singapore.

And Mourinho hopes Ancelotti hands Endrick some game time.

He said: "Just look at who's going [to the Brazil national team] with Carlo. Vinicius [Junior,] who must play; Endrick, and if Carlo plays him, I'll be grateful."

While Mourinho claimed Endrick's future is at Madrid, Endrick was not willing to be so committed.

"I want to be happy, to help my team-mates. To score goals and provide assists, just as I did at Lyon, which was one of the best things that's ever happened to me and one of the happiest times of my life.

"I don't know what will happen in January.

"That's in God's hands. I'm only 20 and I'll see what happens. I just ask to do what's best for my career. "Whatever happens, happens. I'll keep my feet on the ground and do my best. Madrid is a wonderful city, but I'll always do everything I can to further my career."

However, Endrick is enjoying working under Mourinho.

"Mourinho is someone I really trust," Endrick said. "Every day, he comes up to me and asks how I am, but never about football; it's always about my personal life. I really appreciate that.

"He's one of the first managers to come over, have a chat and make me feel very welcome. I'm very grateful for that. I'll always give my all for Real Madrid, which comes before everything else, but with Mourinho's warmth, I give even more."

Konsa to miss England v Croatia as Hill gets senior call-up

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

England will be without Ezri Konsa for Thursday's Nations League clash with Croatia.

Thomas Tuchel was dealt a blow on Monday, when Marc Guehi withdrew from England's squad due to injury.

And now, Guehi's regular central defensive partner Konsa has also now been deemed unavailable for the meeting with Croatia at Wembley.

Konsa has not left the camp, however, with the Arsenal defender aiming to be fit to feature against Spain on Sunday.

With Konsa out against Croatia, Bournemouth's James Hill has earned a first senior call-up.

Congratulations, Hilly Our defender has earned a first senior @England call-up https://t.co/24vtNMgmrs

— AFC Bournemouth (@afcbournemouth) November 10, 2026

Hill has made the most clearances (210) and won the most aerial duels (138) in the Premier League in 2026.

The 24-year-old has won more duels than any other defender in the Premier League this season (71), and his 66% duel success rate is the second-best of any Bournemouth player to contest at least 100 in a top-flight campaign.

Dan Burn was called up as Guehi's replacement, and Tuchel is now likely to have to go with a new-look backline against Croatia.

Jarrad Branthwaite and Jarrel Quansah are England's other fit out-and-out centre-backs, though Ben White is also in the squad.

Wayne Rooney’s I’m a Celebrity Fee Compared to Other Contestants

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Former Manchester United striker Wayne Rooney is set to appear on the 2026 edition of 'I'm a Celebrity... Get Me Out of Here', and the pundit's eye-watering appearance fee has now been revealed.

The hit reality TV show kicks off next week, with the first episode set to air on Monday night on ITV. Fans of the cult classic, hosted by presenting duo Ant and Dec, are avidly awaiting this year's iteration, with football supporters particularly intrigued by how Rooney will fare in the jungle.

The legendary centre-forward, one of the best English footballers of all time, heads down under alongside a host of other stars from different fields, including the likes of Ricky Wilson, Leigh Francis and Chloe Burrows.

Arguably the biggest name in the show's initial group, it's no surprise that Rooney is set to earn substantially more than everyone else travelling to New South Wales next week.

Hanging up his boots in 2021, Rooney has already enjoyed a number of different ventures since retiring from football. He's overseen four separate clubs as a manager, including Derby County, Birmingham City and Plymouth Argyle, while he's also worked extensively as a pundit, mostly for BBC Sport.

His next adventure is now imminent, with the prolific goalscorer set to enter the world of reality TV at the start of next week.

Rooney's wife, Coleen, famously appeared on 'I'm a Celebrity' back in 2024, finishing second behind winner Danny Jones. The former footballer will be looking to go one better than his partner when he heads to Australia.

While his name speaks for itself, Rooney's reserved and down-to-earth persona has sparked huge amounts of interest from the public about how he might deal with the various obstacles that are thrown at contestants in the jungle.

For these reasons, the ex-number nine is set to take home a significant appearance fee, far more than any of the other starting participants.

From Gary Neville to Alex Scott to Mark Chapman, UK pundits are paid very handsomely for the roles of television and radio.

According to SuperTV247 on X, Rooney will be paid £800,000 for his presence on the reality show, a figure that will add to the Manchester United legend's net worth. This is £625,000 more than the next best-paid contestant, Kaiser Chiefs frontman Ricky Wilson.

Behind Rooney and the musician come presenter AJ Odudu, actress Joanna Page and comedian Leigh Francis, all on £150,000. Meanwhile, the lowest earner is set to be internet personality Jack Skipper, who will take home £50,000.

The eyeballs Rooney's antics in the jungle will bring to the show likely justified this enormous fee, although interestingly, Coleen earned a reported £1.5 million for her presence on the 2023 edition. This was on par with politician Nigel Farage's fee in 2023.

Nintendo Shuts Down Switch 2 Jailbreak, Sending Hackers Back To Square One

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

On October 22, news broke that the Switch 2 had been jailbroken for the first time. This meant that it wasâkindaâpossible to mod and dump games on the machine, which in the past would have opened the floodgates to third-party operating systems being installed and an accessible piracy scene. But not this time. Due to the incredibly complicated nature of the jailbreak, the need for now-delisted sofrware SmileBASIC, and that it was software based rather than hardware, Nintendo has been able to shut it down with its latest firmware update.

In the past, Nintendo’s portable consoles have become enormously popular among homebrew developers and the piracy scene for the ease with which they could be adapted. The Nintendo DS (its most successful console until the Switch, selling 154 million units and just shy of a billion games) had the infamous R4 cart onto which ROMs could be installed, while the original model of the Nintendo Switch (157 million units, 1.6 billion games) could be jailbroken due to a hardware issue that Nintendo couldn’t patch out. These exploits didn’t appear to hinder the consoles’ successes, but Nintendo remains resolute in its fury against such actions.

By October 26, hackers had figured out how to get more out of the Switch 2’s very limited exploit, able to dump games as ROMs and dig into the console’s file systems, which would usually be the beginning of emulation plans and homebrew development. Now, however, as reported by Oatmeal Dome, all this is patched out by the device’s 23.5.0 firmware update.

The OS exploit used in the recent jailbreak has been patched in version 23.5.0.

Dumping and modding games will be impossible again from this point forward, with the exception of some cartridge games (and maybe game updates) that are released in the near future.

— OatmealDome (@OatmealDome) November 10, 2026

This is possible because the issues discovered were software based, rather than something intrinsic to the physical hardware inside the Switch 2, as had been the case for the original model of the Switch 1. There, Nintendo had to change the manufacturing, and there was little that could be done to stop Switch 1 users from taking advantage, so long as they didn’t log in to Nintendo’s online services. Of course, of the tens of millions of people who owned the Switch that could do it, vanishingly few actually did, and software sales broke all of Nintendo’s records.

Eventually the crackers will find a way, because eventually they always do, but in this case it could be many years away.

Philippe Clement thinks Norwich City man can follow in Sunderland star’s footsteps

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Norwich City boss Philippe Clement has compared Lucien Mahovo to former PSG and Borussia Dortmund player Thomas Meunier, now a Sunderland star, as the 21-year-old has been deployed in a more attacking role than expected since returning from injury.

Mahovo's emergence into the Norwich fold since he arrived from Notts County back in 2024 has been stunted by various setbacks, most recently an ankle injury during pre-season, which ruled him out for the first couple of months of the campaign.

Since returning to action, though, the 21-year-old has been substituted on as a right-sided attacker in league action, much to the surprise of Canaries fans, as he'd broken through as an attacking left-back.

Clement is honing in on his qualities going forward, while the likes of Ben Chrisene and Darlin Yongwa both battle for the starting left-back spot, and the Norwich boss feels he could thrive in this new role, just like a young Meunier did while he was on the coaching staff at Club Brugge.

It's safe to say that Philippe Clement rates Mahovo highly; he wouldn't be looking for ways to include him while the left-back spot remains pretty saturated otherwise.

Speaking to the press following their recent 5-0 demolition at Bristol City, he explained that, back in Bruges, a young Thomas Meunier was breaking through as an attacker before finding his feet further back.

The 35-year-old, off the back of that, has 83 Belgian caps and many years at the top of the game in Europe with Borussia Dortmund and PSG, before joining Sunderland this past summer and making his bow in the Premier League.

"I spoke with Lucien about the plan in training sessions to put him higher, because I've seen good things with him in four-against-four, five-against-five, six-against-six in the small-sided games about dribbling, shooting, making decisive actions, and like this he has more chance to be in the selection and to come on the pitch, and that's his ambition," Clement said.

"Will it be his position for the rest of his life? I don't know, to be honest. Sometimes those things can happen, and suddenly you see something that changes a career.

"We had Thomas Meunier in Bruges, when I was assistant, as a striker. He became a winger with the manager there, and he did that job defensively well. And later on, he became a full back, and he made an international career, going to the best teams in the world as a right full back.

"He would never have been a Paris (Saint-Germain) player for years as a striker or a right winger, but as a right full back, yes."

Meunier has a career to be proud of, and if Clement is tipping Mahovo to have a similar impact as the 35-year-old has had following a slight positional tweak, then that's high praise.

It's not like Norwich aren't deprived of wingers, either. If the 21-year-old isn't playing at left-back, he'll be competing with Andre Brooks for that right-wing role, whom the Canaries signed for £8 million from Sheffield United in the summer.

The Belgian boss is evidently a big fan, though, and his defensive nous as a winger could help craft a well-rounded player, which would likely put him ahead in the pecking order for the majority of the coaches he'd play under.

"He has so much potential, the boy, and I believe so hard in his potential,” Clement continued. “Also offensively, he does his defensive duty. So I really think he can, in both positions, make an amazing career.

"I believe really hard in Lucien’s quality, not only as a full back but also in those positions to hurt opponents with this playing style, so those are things also to develop.”

Norwich are starting to hit their stride this season, with four successive wins lifting them into the top half at the November international break. Having everyone fit and healthy will be key if the Canaries are to continue to overcome yet another slow start and challenge for the play-offs.

Mahovo has the support and belief from his head coach. Now, he needs to remain available for him to maintain that, something which he's struggled to do now for a couple of years.

In the 2024/25 campaign under Johannes Hoff Thorup, the then-teenage full-back started six consecutive league games between January and February, and looked to be breaking through, before injury curtailed his year.

Mahovo has the potential, both defensively and going forward, but only time will tell whether he can stay healthy enough to reach it.

The Unexpected Benefits of Actually Calling Someone

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Thirty minutes on the old blower is better than therapy.

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

The options for communication are endless. Getting in touch with someone has never been easier, which also means we are subject to frequent digital conversations we'd rather not have. You can thank our overlords in Big Tech for that. Friends, family, enemies, and strangers alike can pepper open inboxes with questions and concerns ranging from the unnecessary to full-scale attacks on your character, tastes, and loosely held opinions. Nearly 90 percent of Americans use text messaging or messaging apps daily, with 29 percent saying they’re texting more than they were one year ago, according to a 2026 YouGov study. Even with notifications turned off, they lurk in the background, and the social pressure to respond is hard for almost anyone to ignore.

But there is an anecdote to this scourge of modern life.

As an older millennial, I am part of the generation that remembers when the internet changed our lives, but I also experienced defining moments using a simple telephone. Even in the infant stages of the internet, the humble telephone, attached via cord that plugged into a wall, was the conduit for good and bad news; it was how we made plans, and how relationships grew and solidified. I spent hours with the handset cradled between my teenage double chin and shoulder, plotting to see bands play, staying up too late talking to girls, and calling phone sex hotlines to see if they were real. (They were.)

What younger generations don’t understand—to their detriment—is that the immediacy and intimacy of the phone call produce different results than a text or direct message. Imagine asking your crush to prom and getting three dots on the screen over and over, your stomach doing backflips as you wait for a yes or no. The phone call is the communication version of getting punched in the face. Your knee-jerk reactions are on display. The ear on the other end of the receiver can sense emotions: anger, reluctance, joy, excitement. You feel all of it innately without FaceTime video.

As adults, there are several instances—even today, especially today—for which a phone call is superior to a text message. One of the greatest is simply catching up with an old friend. Seriously, when’s the last time you cold called a buddy? Thirty minutes on the blower with one of your besties can be exhilarating; you cover far more ground with less wrist pain, and you can do it while driving or sitting as a passenger princess, putting your wired earbuds to good use. Yes, the shock of seeing your name appear might cause a moment of panic in your friend—who died? But they’ll get over it fast, and quickly you’ll fall into an easy conversation. Don’t over think it; just call someone.

In business, the phone call is even more crucial. If you can get someone on the phone, they almost have to give you an answer. Even if it isn’t the one you want to hear, you can move on with your life and stop sending follow-up emails every 48 hours. Efficiency is king, and the phone can help. After all, there’s a reason that in nearly every photo of a captain of industry he or she is walking and talking on a phone. They didn’t get to where they are through text alone. Plus, if you’re under 30, the person on the other end might even be impressed that made the effort top pick up the phone and call. They’ll certainly remember you!

Of course, nostalgia plays a part, but I’m not suggesting you buy an old-school phone and hook it up to the nearest wall. Let’s focus on the practice; you’re iPhone has a calling function. Use it more frequently. Sometimes the newest thing isn’t the best. We have yet to improve on the phone call. Don’t be scared to call someone or to answer when it rings. Pick up and be happy someone is on the other side. That’s a blessing in its own right.

Arsenal’s Havertz penalty controversy sparks fresh call for change to football laws

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal’s controversial penalty claim against Hull City has raised new questions about how the offside rule is interpreted in football.

Former Premier League official Dermot Gallagher believes that whilst he understands why the officials made the decision, the result would have been different under the current rules.

Speaking on Ref Watch on Sky Sports, Gallagher said that the holding should have been the main focus of the incident involving Kai Havertz and Hull defender Nobel Mendy.

His comments have increased criticism of referee Farai Hallam and VAR official Michael Oliver. However, Gallagher also feels that the language used in the laws needs to be addressed to avoid this issue in the future.

The incident happened shortly before half-time during Arsenal’s 1-0 victory over Hull at the Emirates Stadium.

Mendy pulled Havertz’s shirt inside the penalty area, prompting VAR to recommend an on-field review. Hallam subsequently confirmed that the Hull defender had committed a holding offence.

However, he ruled that Havertz was in an offside position and awarded Hull a free-kick.

Gallagher believes that was incorrect under the laws of the game. He explained that being in an offside position does not automatically constitute an offence.

The player must be involved in play before the referee can give a penalty. In this instance, Havertz had not tried to play the ball or challenge an opponent before being pulled back by Mendy.

This means that the player was held first, so a penalty should have been given to Arsenal.

Gallagher said that the AR had first raised their flag for offside. He said that it was a great process with VAR, but the initial decision could have affected the outcome.

He recognised why the decision was made even though he felt Arsenal should have been awarded the penalty.

In terms of common sense, he said it does not look right to award the penalty, as Havertz was in an offside position and couldn’t legally contest for the ball.

But he emphasised that the officials must strictly enforce the laws of the game. Gallagher thinks there is some grey space here which can be addressed by having more specific wording.

This could be crucial in these types of situations as the difference between being in an offside position and committing an offside offence can have a big effect on whether a penalty is awarded.

It’s a small comfort for Arsenal after missing the chance to take the lead from the spot. But Martin Odegaard did eventually score the winner in the 72nd minute to give Mikel Arteta’s men three points.

The incident has exposed a wider problem for referees. Until the laws provide clearer guidance, similar decisions could continue to generate controversy.

Researchers designed a stiff new solid that keeps heat from escaping better than soft foams and aerogels

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A solid crystal film with organic layers blocks heat nearly as well as still air, while staying stiffer than most plastics, a record for dense solids.

The best heat blockers are mostly made of air. Inside foam, it sits trapped in tiny pockets, which is why foam insulates so well and also why it squashes so easily. Now, engineers and chemists have made a solid crystal film that is stiff and has no air pockets at all.

It blocks heat nearly as well as still air, while staying stiffer than most plastics.

According to the team, no fully dense solid had ever been measured blocking heat this well at room temperature. The result suggests stiffness and insulation don’t have to be traded against each other.

Jun Liu, an engineer at North Carolina State University, led the work with physicist Dali Sun. Chemists at the University of North Carolina at Chapel Hill and colleagues in China also took part.

So far the stiff crystal film exists only in the lab. It is a layer far thinner than a human hair on silicon wafers, and it contains lead.

In most materials, stiffness and heat flow rise together. Strong bonds hold diamond together, and it is extremely rigid, and it carries heat very well. Plastics are made of loosely stuck chains, which makes them softer and poorer at passing heat along.

“Because, in general, stiff materials are good at conducting heat, and materials that are not stiff are good at insulating against heat,” Sun said.

That’s why most insulation is soft. Foams and aerogels get close to air by being mostly air. However, they give up almost all their stiffness to do it.

Better insulation is one way to save energy in buildings, but a coating that has to hold its shape on a circuit or a solar panel can’t be that fragile.

The stiff film belongs to a family of layered crystals called inorganic-organic perovskites that stack thin sheets of lead and iodine between layers of organic, carbon-based molecules.

In earlier work, Liu’s group found that swapping some of the carbon-carbon chains in the organic layers with benzene rings – stiff, six-sided rings of carbon atoms – made the crystals both stiffer and worse at carrying heat.

This time the team used molecules carrying two rings joined by a double bond. The rings make the layer rigid. They also vibrate mostly at high frequencies, which barely overlap with the vibrations of the lead-iodide sheets, so heat has trouble crossing from one layer to the next.

The longer molecules also jumble the way the sheets stack. That disorder slows the passage of heat even more.

The team compared four versions. The one with a plain carbon chain carried about five times the heat as the best version, which used a molecule called azobenzene ethyl ammonium.

To test the films, the researchers coated each one with a thin layer of metal. A short laser pulse heated the surface, and a second beam tracked how fast that heat drained away into the film below.

At room temperature, the best film carried heat only about 60 percent better than still air. That’s less than half as well as typical plastics, and one spot on one sample came in lower still.

The team got its best results from films about 6 millionths of an inch (150 nanometers) thick. Thinner and thicker films did worse, and only films in a narrow band of thicknesses stayed near the record.

Measurements at several spots on several samples gave consistent results, so the team doesn’t think it was a fluke of one sample.

For the second test, the team pressed a tiny vibrating probe against the film to measure how much it resisted bending. The azobenzene film came out stiffer than most plastics and far stiffer than foams and aerogels.

The films are made by spinning a liquid onto a surface and then heating it. The authors wrote that the method can be adapted to coat large or flexible surfaces cheaply. This is in contrast to earlier, ultralow-heat films that needed a high-vacuum chamber.

In an interview with Earth.com, Liu picked out two early targets for this technology. “Electronics and spacecraft would be the first series of applications, for the near-room-temperature thermal insulation in the form of thin film coating,” he said.

The team also sees it as a coating for use near room temperature, based on how it held up when heated.

The researchers see other possible roles too, such as shielding parts in fuel cells and solar collectors, or slowing the heat that spreads when a battery fails. None of those uses has been tested.

Like the materials in many experimental perovskite solar cells, the film is built around lead.

“Yes, the current formula has lead in it and there needs to be environmental considerations if the exact composition will be used,” Liu told Earth.com.

He called the tested film an example of a design idea, and he pointed to related crystals made without lead as the route to practical use. Handling lead safely is already a question for perovskite solar recycling.

The team also spelled out limits of the design idea. A near-twin molecule, with the two rings joined by carbon atoms instead of nitrogen, didn’t jumble the layers nearly as much and missed the record.

The team’s model accounts for that gap only loosely, and it can’t yet predict which molecule will work before someone makes it.

Nobody has shown whether a lead-free crystal keeps the same trick. By the team’s own estimate, a molecule whose rings twist more easily could push the film’s heat flow close to that of air itself. Making one is the next test.

The full study was published in the journal Science Advances.

Steam's new Steam Deck framerate graphs are good news for handheld players, though I wouldn't fully trust the numbers yet

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Valve have finally made clear what they've been doing with all that hoovered-up performance data and all those "Framerate Estimator" text strings: a framerate estimator for the Steam Deck. Or, rather, a framerate average-r, as games on the Steam Store are now getting attached graphs – under their existing Steam Deck Compatibility details – that show how many frames-per-secondseses that game has been recording on real players' handhelds, the OLED and original LCD models both.

The thinking, as Valve explain in a Steam post, is to set more precise performance expectations than the existing verification ratings – Verified, Playable, and Unsupported – provide. This may induce some gentle learned nodding, if you've ever tried an Unsupported game only to find it works more or less fine, or have ever wondered why a game that can just about scrape 30fps together gets the same green good-job sticker as something that screams along at 90fps. Now, when checking that rating, you'll also get a relatively specific idea of smoothness and/or chugging potential.

Or will you? By Valve's own admission, the displayed results may be skewed by V-Sync treachery, where this anti-screen tear setting (which many games enable by default) could force games into briefly running at unrepresentative framerates. The scoundrel. My current handheld game beau, Mortal Shell II, looks like it may be falling victim to this: its results for the LCD Steam Deck show it mostly runs around 30fps, which is in line with my experience, but the OLED results show it averaging around 45fps. The Steam Deck OLED's APU is a tiny bit quicker than the LCD Deck's, but it's not that much of an upgrade.

There's work to be done, then, though I do appreciate the concept. As tightly as I cling to my battery-efficient, mercifully Windows-free Deck OLED in the face of drastically more powerful handhelds, with every year it becomes a little less likely that new games will comfortably run on it. Verification ratings can still act as decent (if broad) guidelines, but even with V-Sync putting an immaculately untorn thumb on the scales, game-specific framerate data will be much more illustrative.

As for whether these histograms will also get Steam Machine versions, or perhaps cover non-Valve handhelds, Valve say this: "We see this update as a stepping stone toward providing framerate data for more devices than just Steam Deck. To this end, we are also prompting customers to opt-in to providing anonymized framerate data in the Steam desktop client. This opt-in is currently only on Steam Client Beta, and will ship to all customers in the future." So, maybe, if you gizzus yer data.

GTA 6 Presales Are 'Running Far in Excess of Our Expectations,' Take-Two Interactive CEO Says

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

GTA 6 is so hot, it's hurting Take-Two's other games

Grand Theft Auto 6 is one of the most anticipated releases of this decade, so it's no surprise that it's selling like hotcakes before it's even out. In this fiscal quarter's earnings presentation, the CEO of developer Take-Two Interactive revealed that pre-orders "are tracking far above initial expectations". However, GTA 6's success might also be the reason Take-Two's other game series failed to meet target sales.

For many, the world will stop on Nov. 19 as gamers enter Leonida for the first time. Players across the globe are preparing to use vacation days for the launch of GTA 6. Some companies are closing stores so their employees can play. Even the United States Army is granting soldiers four days of leave to experience this one-of-a-kind release. The hype around the release of Grand Theft Auto 6 is unprecedented.

Pre-sales for GTA 6 went live on Jun. 25, giving players nearly five months to pre-order the game, and it's going even better than anticipated. Take-Two Interactive's CEO, Strauss Zelnick, told Variety that pre-sales are "running far in excess of [company] expectations." While Rockstar Games has yet to confirm these numbers, reports from earlier this month indicated that Grand Theft Auto 6 had sold 8 million copies ahead of launch.

In the fiscal report, Zelnick stated that with pre-orders continuing to "reach unprecedented levels," Take-Two expects to deliver "a historic year." Despite this, the release of GTA 6 might have hindered other Take-Two releases. It's no secret that many publishers deliberately avoided launching their games in November this year, as they knew most eyes would be on GTA 6. That is why so many games came out in September, and as a result, Take-Two's NBA 2K27, which launched on Sept. 9, fell through the cracks and failed to meet sales expectations.

While Zelnick hoped for better results from NBA 2K27, he told Bloomberg that "if your lunch is going to be eaten, I'd rather be the one eating it," fully aware that this is a case of "suffering from success." Even though the sales numbers were below company expectations, it was still the second-biggest launch for Take-Two's basketball game.

Rockstar's new Grand Theft Auto game is on pace to make up to $5.2 billion at launch

Core inflation excludes oil. Bitcoin investors still cannot ignore it

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Stronger post-2020 oil transmission could complicate monetary easing, but second-round effects have not yet appeared.

Oil could remain an obstacle to lower borrowing costs and greater risk appetite among Bitcoin investors, even when energy is excluded from core inflation. A Federal Reserve staff study published Nov. 9 estimates a stronger link between oil-price changes and underlying inflation in data from 2021 onward, potentially complicating monetary easing.

The findings put numbers to a channel behind the financing risks discussed in CryptoSlate's recent diesel coverage. They describe a conditional inflation threat: the authors say second-round effects from the current oil shock have not yet materialized.

Excluding energy does not remove every fuel cost embedded in other products. The U.K.'s Office for National Statistics explains that core CPI strips out direct energy components while retaining indirect energy costs in prices such as hospitality, telecommunications and transportation. Businesses can therefore pass energy costs into prices outside the excluded categories.

The Fed researchers pooled Canada, the euro area, Japan, the United Kingdom and the United States, using data from 2000 through the second quarter of 2026. The U.S. measure is core personal consumption expenditures inflation; the others use core consumer price inflation.

For the period beginning in 2021, the model links a 10-percentage-point increase in relative oil-price inflation to a roughly 0.14-percentage-point increase in core inflation. In the pre-2021 period, the estimated response was about 0.01 percentage point and statistically insignificant.

Those units matter. Relative oil-price inflation measures quarterly changes in local-currency Brent prices relative to core inflation. A 10-percentage-point increase in that measure is different from a 10% rise in oil's price level. The model allows transmission through the current quarter and four previous quarters.

The Fed authors call their forward inflation paths an upper bound rather than a central prediction, assuming elevated persistence and pass-through continue through 2028. Their pooled estimates may also capture wider post-pandemic shocks rather than a lasting change in oil's influence. These are staff findings, not official U.S. inflation forecasts.

In a separate structural simulation, the Fed staff find that stronger oil transmission combined with more persistent inflation requires materially tighter policy even as activity weakens.

That creates a possible headwind for Bitcoin through investors' willingness to take risk. An August 2023 IMF working paper found that U.S. monetary tightening reduced a common crypto-price factor through this risk-taking channel.

The Bitcoin implication is an inference from the two studies: if oil sustains core-price pressure, it could delay financing relief and constrain risk appetite. The studies do not estimate a Bitcoin price response.

The BLS schedule lists the October CPI report for Nov. 10. Persistent core pressure accompanied by reduced easing expectations would support the financing-risk interpretation; sustained disinflation and improving easing expectations would weaken it.

Prediction: Albania vs Finland

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Verdict: Albania to win Best odds: 1.91 Bookmaker: 1xBet

Albania welcomes Finland to Tirana on Thursday with first place still within reach for three teams in Group C1. The host leads the standings with nine points, two ahead of both Finland and Belarus, and has an opportunity to take one rival out of the race for automatic promotion before the final round. Finland, however, already has a win over Albania in this campaign.

Three victories from four games have put Albania in control of its own promotion prospects. Rolando Maran’s team opened with a 2-0 home win over Belarus, followed that with a 3-0 victory in San Marino and recovered from its defeat in Finland by beating San Marino 2-1 in Tirana.

That last result was closer than Albania would have wanted, particularly against an opponent it had beaten comfortably away from home. Even so, taking maximum points from both home fixtures has given Maran’s side something solid to build on before its toughest test in Tirana so far.

The circumstances of October’s 2-1 defeat in Helsinki deserve attention. Rey Manaj put Albania ahead after four minutes, but goalkeeper Etrit Berisha was dismissed in the 22nd minute. Leo Walta subsequently scored twice from free kicks, turning the match around while the visitor spent more than an hour a player short.

That early dismissal limits how much we can read into the result when assessing Thursday’s return fixture. Albania had made the better start and now has home advantage, although Maran will want greater efficiency in front of goal. The coach highlighted finishing as an area for improvement in his November squad announcement, and Finland’s defensive record suggests his players cannot expect a long succession of clear chances.

Thomas Strakosha remains unavailable as he continues his recovery, leaving Etrit Berisha, Alen Sherri and Mario Dajsinani as the goalkeepers in the squad. Dinamo City right-back Ysni Ismaili has received his first senior call-up.

Armando Broja, Anis Mehmeti and Marash Kumbulla have not been selected. Maran still has several attacking options, with Manaj, Mirlind Daku, Myrto Uzuni and Cristian Shpendi included. Kristjan Asllani, Ylber Ramadani and Qazim Laçi are also available in midfield.

Finland arrives with a healthy goal difference and a less convincing attacking record once the individual results are examined.

Seven of its nine group goals came in the away victory over San Marino. The other two were scored against Albania after Berisha’s dismissal, while both meetings with Belarus ended without a Finnish goal. A goalless home draw was followed by a 1-0 away defeat in October, costing Jacob Friis’ team ground in the promotion race.

There is more encouragement at the other end of the pitch. Finland has conceded only twice in four group matches, a record that should give it confidence in its ability to keep Thursday’s contest close. Albania may have to be patient, especially if the opening exchanges produce few opportunities.

Finland finishes the group at home to San Marino, so a positive result in Tirana would leave it with an appealing final fixture. A victory would also take it above Albania. Finding enough attacking quality against a direct rival remains the main concern, although the November squad gives Friis more options than he had during the first four rounds.

Oliver Antman and Naatan Skyttä return after missing Finland’s opening four Nations League matches. Their availability should give Friis more flexibility in the attacking positions.

Robert Ivanov and Fredrik Jensen are also back after longer absences, while midfielder Luka Hyryläinen has earned his first senior call-up. Walta remains in the squad alongside forwards Joel Pohjanpalo and Benjamin Källman, with Lukas Hradecky among the goalkeeping options.

Finland’s defensive record and returning attackers make this a demanding assignment for Albania. There is enough quality in the visiting squad to punish another careless foul or defensive mistake.

Nevertheless, Albania’s home results offer the stronger foundation for a selection. Finland’s difficulties against Belarus also raise doubts about how consistently it can create chances against a well-organized opponent. We expect a close match, with home advantage helping Albania find the decisive goal.

How to Stop Birdseed Getting Wet in Feeders This Fall – and Why it's Vital You Bother

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Beautiful plants and ideas to brighten your garden.

The Clever Way to Reuse Old Tomato Cages to Protect Your Plants From Freezing Temperatures

Sorry Squirrels, This Simple Trick Stops Pests Eating Tulip Bulbs (and it Beats Chicken Wire Every Time)

6 Gardening Jobs You Must Finish Before the First Hard Freeze – Or You’ll Regret it in Spring

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Find out why soggy birdseed may harm your feathered friends, when it's safe to dry damp seed, and how to keep birdfood dry through fall and winter.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A rainy week in fall can turn a full feeder into a clump of wet, sour seed that's unsafe for birds to eat and a waste of your hard-earned money. A few simple changes to your bird feeding set-up to keep seed dry, however, keeps both your feathered friends and your bank balance a lot happier.

A backyard feeder can still look full from the kitchen window after a couple of rainy days in a row, but get closer and you'll see the seed inside has darkened and packed into a wet plug above the ports. In some cases, you can dry out the seed, but soggy birdseed costs more than the price of the bag. Damp seed quickly grows mold and fungi, some that release harmful toxins that can cause fatal infections in these beneficial garden friends. Wet grain also gives harmful bacteria an easy start and parasites a place to survive, turning a crowded feeder into a disease hotspot. Some seed decays into a mush that small birds can't safely digest, too.

So, knowing when it's safe to dry bird seed, and how to keep it from getting soaked in the first place, is an essential part of feeding birds through fall and winter.

Good seed isn't cheap and you'll find plenty of bird-feeding advice online recommending spreading wet grains on a tray to dry out. That suggestion comes with a catch, however. If the birdseed got damp in a recent rainfall just after you filled the feeder an hour ago, then go ahead and dry it out so you can feed it to your garden birds. However, seed that's been sitting soaked inside a feeder or has been out in all weathers for days is likely contaminated, and there's no good evidence that drying makes contaminated seed safe again.

You see, if a bag of birdseed just got wet on the garage floor an hour ago, a gust blew rain across the scoop on the way to the feeder, or the bag sat open by the door during a drizzle, you know that seed is likely still clean. But seed in a feeder is a different story: birds have been perching on it and leaving droppings, harmful bacteria may be multiplying in the damp conditions, or parasites and pathogens may be present. So, the safer call is always to throw soggy birdseed out.

If you're confident the birdseed is clean and only recently wetted, spread it on a clean baking tray or fine mesh screen, one seed deep if you can, and leave it to dry indoors in a warm room where the air moves. A rimmed baker's pan like this from Walmart keeps seed from rolling off. Stir the seed occasionally and, once it pours loosely again, it's safe to feed. If the seed smells at all musty or shows any fuzz while it's drying, though, toss it out.

It's an easy call to throw out birdseed if it's moldy, clumped together, smells musty or sour, or looks darker or discolored compared to when it went into the feeder. But even if the seed still looks normal, it may still carry mold or bacteria which can affect bird health – fungi can grow inside a seed's shell and often there's nothing on the outside to give it away. So, if you ever find yourself wondering is wet birdseed safe, bag it up for the trash and keep your backyard birds safe.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Bird experts recommend cleaning a bird feeder every 1-2 weeks in winter, and it's wise to clean it whenever it gets soaked through, too. It's a good idea to give your feeders a quick look after any heavy rain: if the seed doesn't shift freely when you tip the feeder to one side, it needs cleaning. The more often you clean a feeder, the quicker and easier the job becomes, especially if you use diluted white vinegar to clean bird feeders, so it needn't be a chore.

Empty out the wet seed, take the feeder apart and scrub each piece in hot, soapy water. It's easier with a long brush with straight and curved ends like this Songbird Essentials 2-in-1 feeder cleaning brush from Walmart. Clear each drainage hole with a toothpick, too. Rinse thoroughly then let every piece dry completely before refilling and hanging up the feeder. Rake up any wet seed and hulls under the feeder as well.

Of course, the best solution to one of the most common bird-feeding problems is to prevent the seed getting wet in the first place. Many hanging feeders come with a dome or rain guard that sits above the seed tube to keep the worst of the rain off the ports, but they're rarely big enough to provide effective protection. Adding a more robust layer of protection, like this bird feeder rain guard from Amazon that comes in either a 16-inch or 19-inch diameter design with a snap hook to clip the feeder on underneath, is far more effective and doubles as a squirrel baffle, too. For a hopper feeder, look for a design with a roof that overhangs the ports and has drainage holes in the base or, on a tray feeder, a mesh bottom that lets rainwater run straight through.

Keep feeders away from roof edges and gutters where runoff pours straight down, and during wet spells, only put out as much seed as birds will finish quickly. Keep birdseed dry in storage, too, by using a lidded container that mice can't chew into like Behrens Galvanized Steel Can from Home Depot.

Making sure you don't leave soggy birdseed in feeders is an important part of helping backyard birds survive winter. Just like taking steps to stop your bird bath freezing over, this is an easy job for us gardeners that's hugely beneficial for our feathered friends. There are so many ways to feed birds through fall and winter, from planting a berry-bearing shrub to feed birds through winter to saving kitchen scraps and using the lard-smear trick that makes sure smaller songbirds get their fair share of food, and it's a joy to help our garden visitors. But if you're choosing to offer a nutritious supply of grains, do make sure you're not serving up any fatal fungal spores alongside by keeping birdseed dry.

With a microbiology background focused on fungi, he’s spent over a decade solving tough and intricate gardening problems. After spinal injuries and brain surgery, Tyler’s approach to gardening changed. It became less about the hobby and more about recovery and adapting to physical limits. His growing success shows that disability doesn’t have to stop you from your goals.

James Gunn’s Exit from DC Studios Was Due to Tensions with Skydance's Executives, Report Says

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

It’s been just over four years since James Gunn and Peter Safran took the reins at DC Studios as co-CEOs. For fans impressed by Gunn’s Marvel films, including Guardians of the Galaxy, the prospect of what Gunn and Safran could bring to DC comics adaptations across film, animation, and television was especially exciting.

However, Gunn and Safran’s full vision may never reach fruition, and now, thanks to The Hollywood Reporter, we have a plausible explanation.

Soon after Skydance acquired Warner Bros. Discovery, major changes swept through Warner Bros., including a restructuring and layoffs. Warner Bros. heads Pam Abdy and Mike De Luca were also let go. Gunn and Safran were believed to have escaped such a fate. That was the case, at least until Skydance co-CEOs David Ellison and Ynon Kreiz arrived at Gunn and Safran’s offices on the Warner Bros. lot on Nov. 6, marking a turning point for DC Studios.

According to The Hollywood Reporter’s sources, Gunn and Safran were told that, despite being previously reassured their roles would be safe under the new merger, Skydance’s co-CEOs had changed their minds. Though Gunn and Safran are no longer CEOs of DC Studios, Warner Bros. Discovery did announce that the two will “ensure a smooth, collaborative transition” of the studio to new leadership: Jeremy Latcham, previously a key member of Marvel Studios’ executive team.

The Hollywood Reporter's insider revealed that Latcham’s move into Gunn and Safran’s role had been in the works since early fall, when Skydance Motion Picture Group co-chairs Josh Greenstein and Dana Goldberg approached him. The situation is particularly damning because other sources point to “several points of tension” between Greenstein and Goldberg and Gunn and Safran. Before the merger, Gunn and Safran reported directly to Warner Bros. CEO David Zaslav and had tight control over DC’s product rollout, spanning movies, TV shows, and animation.

Reportedly, this didn’t work particularly well for Greenstein and Goldberg, who were trying to consolidate all motion pictures under their leadership. Furthermore, Gunn and Safran were reportedly “too slow” to share plans, details, or even footage with Skydance’s leadership.

The Hollywood Reporter also mentions that Skydance's new leadership wasn't partiuclarly impressed with the results achieved by DC Studios under Gunn and Safran's guidance. Their most successful movie, Superman, which grossed $618 million, "did not net out," according to The Hollywood Reporter, and failed to achieve the kind of pop culture impact the executives expected. While Clayface grossed over $100 million on a relatively small budget, Supergirl was "a disaster in the eyes of the new regime," The Hollywood Reporter's sources say.

The main reason Gunn and Safran’s vision for DC Studios failed will be debated for years. For now, though, the answer seems to be studio politics and business results. It’s a sad end to their leadership, but one that, if box-office numbers are any indication, we ultimately saw coming.

Mauricio Pochettino Already Has His Own Dele Alli at Man Utd in Shea Lacey

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Manchester United are set to sack Michael Carrick as manager, and Mauricio Pochettino is a leading contender to replace him in the dugout.

GIVEMESPORT sources understand the Argentine, who is currently the manager of the United States national team and signed a new contract until 2030 during the summer, has a release clause worth around $10m (£7.4m), and he is one of the top candidates to replace the iconic former midfielder.

United have won just one of their ten Premier League games this season and find themselves in 18th place following a dramatic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa prior to the international break, making a change of manager a necessity, despite Carrick only signing a two-year contract at the end of last season.

Pochettino is someone that has been looked at for several years at Manchester United as a potential manager. He was considered as an option to replace Ole Gunnar Solskjaer, Erik ten Hag and Ruben Amorim, but the club always opted for alternative options.

However, it now seems as though a deal could be done to bring the 54-year-old into Old Trafford. Pochettino is best known for his time at Tottenham, where he built a squad that reached the Champions League final and finished second in the Premier League.

The keys to that team was the foursome of Christian Eriksen, Heung-Min Son, Dele Alli, and Harry Kane, who were the most important cogs in the machine and regularly combined to win games for the team. The former PSG star is always keen to rebuild those relationships and find players wherever he goes who can take up that responsibility.

At PSG he had Messi, Neymar, and Mbappe. At Chelsea he had Cole Palmer and Nicolas Jackson working in tandem ahead of Enzo Fernandez and Moises Caicedo. At Man Utd he has got an abundance of quality in attack, but there is one star who he could be ready to hand a big opportunity too in the starting lineup.

Carrick has so far preferred to use Amad, Matheus Cunha, Bryan Mbeumo, Bruno Fernandes and Benjamin Sesko as his forward options, while Marcus Rashford featured heavily too before his recent hip issue. But in recent weeks, youngster Shea Lacey has been turning in some of the more impressive performances.

Against AS Roma he came off the bench to supply an assist as the team came from 2-0 down to win 3-2, and in his only start this season against Brighton in the EFL Cup he scored the opening goal. Now it's time for him to be unleashed more regularly, and Pochettino is the perfect coach for that to happen.

Lacey is one of the most talented young academy stars to come out of Carrington in recent years, and it's clear that he is now ready to make an impact in the Premier League and Champions League.

The number ten role is taken by Bruno Fernandes, but with Amad having not scored a goal for almost an entire calendar year and Benjamin Sesko sidelined until the new year with a shin injury, there is a huge chance for Lacey to step up.

Much like Dele was at Spurs, Lacey is young, hungry, and incredibly talented. He has the technical attributes to hang at the top level, has the eye for a killer pass, knows how to finish, and is more than capable of handling himself physically too.

Pochettino has a reputation for using young players and helping to develop them, and Lacey could be the perfect project for him. If their relationship becomes anything like the one Poch and Dele had, then this could be the start of something magical at Old Trafford.

I found a hidden Android tool that solves all my tiny text troubles

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Tech > Services & Software > Operating Systems > Mobile OS > Android

When the print on your Android display is too small to read, and the pinch-to-zoom feature isn't working as expected, try this.

How many times have you been on your phone, wanted to read something (a piece of news, a website, etc.), only to find the text is too small and every effort to zoom in doesn’t work?

Also: 7 Android accessibility features that can make your life easier

This happens quite often when I’m viewing content in the Google News app — especially in the morning, when my eyes are still in “wake up” mode. It can be frustrating. And it’s not only text. Sometimes there’s an image I need to enlarge, but for whatever reason, pinch-to-zoom isn’t working on that particular screen.

Fortunately, there’s an option you can enable to ensure you can always zoom in on your Android device, no matter what’s on the screen. The option is found in Accessibility and is aptly called Magnification. With this feature enabled, you can quickly zoom in on the screen to enlarge any displayed content. It doesn’t matter what’s on the screen or what app is showing the image, page, or text — Magnification will magnify it.

This iteration of the feature has been available since Android 15. I found it by accident while poking around the Settings page. Since then, I’ve used it quite a bit, and it’s eased my morning frustration with reading news sites.

The Magnification feature is quite simple: once enabled, a magnifier shortcut appears on the screen. Tap that button, and whatever is on your screen will increase in size. It’s really that easy. One caveat: As long as the Magnification shortcut is enabled, the button remains on your screen. You can move the button up and down or to the left or right side of the display, but if you want the feature enabled, the button must remain.

The good news, however, is that the button does fade a bit, so it’s not glaring and in your face. You can still see it, but it gets just enough transparency that it doesn’t stand out all that much.

Also: Android is getting my favorite iOS tracking feature

Open Settings by dragging the Notification Shade down twice, then tapping the gear icon (or tapping Settings in the App Drawer). Where this is located depends on the Android version you’re using and your device. For example, on my Pixel 9 Pro, it’s in Settings > Accessibility > Magnification. On a RedMagic 12 Pro, it’s in Settings > Features > Accessibility. If you don’t find it right away, open Settings and search for Magnification.

Once you’ve found the Magnification option, tap the On/Off slider until it’s in the On position, and the Magnifier button will appear on your screen. Tap the button, and whatever is on the display will enlarge enough that you shouldn’t have any trouble reading it.

Here’s a little tip: After tapping the Magnification button, whatever’s been enlarged can be scrolled vertically, but not horizontally. Fortunately, there’s a way to ensure that you can read everything on the page.

Also: I use Google Magnifier to read tiny print without my glasses

Tap the gear icon at the bottom right of the display to open the options pop-up. On this page, tap the “Magnifier size” icon that’s second from the right. This enables a movable magnifier you can place where you need it (kind of like a traditional magnifying glass). You can also change the size of the movable magnifier (by tapping one of the other “Magnifier size” buttons) as well as the size of the text when magnified (by dragging the bottom slider).

Also: RedMagic 12 Pro is the fastest Android phone I’ve ever seen

This feature has alleviated the early-morning frustration of having to squint (even with my glasses on) to read certain news sites. Give it a try and see if it doesn’t make your Android life a bit clearer.

Shiba Inu (SHIB) Scammers Fake 'Ryoshi' to Push 137 Scam Tokens and Dupe Investors

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

According to longtime SHIB community member Mazrael, since the SHIB deployer wallet went silent in October 2021, at least 137 scam tokens have falsely claimed that the SHIB deployer sent them the coins. The intent of scammers is to 'fake' Shiba Inu's pseudonymous founder, "Ryoshi," and the reason behind this is to appear legitimate: If "Ryoshi" sent it, people trust it.

Mazrael explains how this trick works: a token is just a program, and it keeps its own record of transfers. The scammer can write any address into that record as the sender. So these scammers write the SHIB deployer's address, and on Etherscan it looks like "Ryoshi" sent the token. However, the real SHIB deployer (0xB8f2…FA08) hasn't sent a single transaction since October 2021.

How scammers fake "Ryoshi" Since the SHIB deployer wallet went silent in October 2021, at least 137 scam tokens have pretended it sent them their coins. Why? To look legitimate. If "Ryoshi" sent it, people trust it. How the trick works: A token is just a program, and it keeps its own record of transfers. The scammer can write ANY address into that record as the sender. So they write the SHIB deployer's address, and on Etherscan it looks like "Ryoshi" sent you their token. But the deployer never signed anything. Click the transaction itself and look at "From". That's the wallet that really sent it, and it's never the SHIB deployer. The numbers: - 9,131+ fake transfers - 2,500+ wallets hit - 0 signed by the deployer Some of the fakes: APE, three "SHIBA INU2.0" tokens, ShibChain, SHIB DAO, METARUFFY, a token literally called "Ryoshi", Caw and Baby Caw, three fake "Shibarium" tokens (a year before the real one), "BONE2.0" and three "UNLEASHED DAO" tokens. Many got shilled by OGs and ex-admins as "Ryoshi-backed" to make them look legit. The real SHIB deployer (0xB8f2…FA08) hasn't sent a single transaction since October 2021. Always check who signed the transaction, not what the token says.

'The SHIB deployer never signed anything,' Mazrael stated, urging the crypto community to click on related token transactions and examine the "From" address, saying this is the wallet that sent it and was never the SHIB deployer.

Mazrael shared the numbers, outlining over 9,131 fake transfers, over 2,500 wallets hit, and zero signed by the SHIB deployer. He went on to name some of these fake tokens, adding that some of these tokens got promoted as "Ryoshi-backed" to make them look legitimate.

This disclosure warns crypto users to always check who signed the transaction, not what the token says.

In a separate post, Mazrael explained that nothing links the SHIB deployer address to any person.

The SHIB deployer (0xB8f2…FA08) only deployed one contract: SHIB, and it hasn't sent a single transaction since October 2021. It has no public signed message on record, either on-chain or on Etherscan.

LEASH, BONE, and TREAT each came from different deployers. Mazrael indicated that BONE's deployer is shiba-swap.eth, whereas LEASH came from '0xA221…1494,' the same key that deployed StarLink, whose own site names Woof Decentra as LEASH's developer. That key kept LEASH's rebase oracle, an admin key, for five years.
